        <div data-pjax="true" data-test-selector="body-content" data-view-component="true" class="markdown-body tmp-my-3"><h2>⭐️ Highlights</h2>
<p>Haystack 3.0 is a major release for building <strong>production-grade agents with full control and flexibility</strong>. It ships a wave of new capabilities: a more capable <code>Agent</code> with hooks and first-class skills, built-in run introspection, first-class async for serving, a leaner core, and safer pipeline loading.</p>
<p>A few small, intentional breaking changes come with it but our <a href="https://docs.haystack.deepset.ai/docs/migration" rel="nofollow">Migration Guide</a> and <strong>Upgrading to Haystack 3.0</strong> make it easy to upgrade.</p>
<h3>🤖 A more capable, adaptable <code>Agent</code></h3>
<p>The <code>Agent</code> gained a general-purpose <strong>hooks system</strong> and now owns tool execution end to end (the standalone <code>ToolInvoker</code> has been <strong>removed</strong>). Hooks at <code>before_run</code>, <code>before_llm</code>, <code>before_tool</code>, <code>after_tool</code>, <code>on_exit</code>, and <code>after_run</code> let you shape behavior, enforce guardrails, and add human-in-the-loop checkpoints without touching the agent's internals:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-python notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="from haystack.components.agents import Agent
from haystack.components.generators.chat import OpenAIChatGenerator
from haystack.hooks import hook

@hook
def audit_tool_calls(state):
    pending = state.data[&quot;messages&quot;][-1].tool_calls
    print(f&quot;about to run: {[tc.tool_name for tc in pending]}&quot;)

agent = Agent(
    chat_generator=OpenAIChatGenerator(),
    tools=[...],
    hooks={&quot;before_tool&quot;: [audit_tool_calls]},
)"><pre><span class="pl-k">from</span> <span class="pl-s1">haystack</span>.<span class="pl-s1">components</span>.<span class="pl-s1">agents</span> <span class="pl-k">import</span> <span class="pl-v">Agent</span>
<span class="pl-k">from</span> <span class="pl-s1">haystack</span>.<span class="pl-s1">components</span>.<span class="pl-s1">generators</span>.<span class="pl-s1">chat</span> <span class="pl-k">import</span> <span class="pl-v">OpenAIChatGenerator</span>
<span class="pl-k">from</span> <span class="pl-s1">haystack</span>.<span class="pl-s1">hooks</span> <span class="pl-k">import</span> <span class="pl-s1">hook</span>

<span class="pl-en">@<span class="pl-s1">hook</span></span>
<span class="pl-k">def</span> <span class="pl-en">audit_tool_calls</span>(<span class="pl-s1">state</span>):
    <span class="pl-s1">pending</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-s1">state</span>.<span class="pl-c1">data</span>[<span class="pl-s">"messages"</span>][<span class="pl-c1">-</span><span class="pl-c1">1</span>].<span class="pl-c1">tool_calls</span>
    <span class="pl-en">print</span>(<span class="pl-s">f"about to run: <span class="pl-s1"><span class="pl-kos">{</span>[<span class="pl-s1">tc</span>.<span class="pl-c1">tool_name</span> <span class="pl-k">for</span> <span class="pl-s1">tc</span> <span class="pl-c1">in</span> <span class="pl-s1">pending</span>]<span class="pl-kos">}</span></span>"</span>)

<span class="pl-s1">agent</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-en">Agent</span>(
    <span class="pl-s1">chat_generator</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-en">OpenAIChatGenerator</span>(),
    <span class="pl-s1">tools</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span>[...],
    <span class="pl-s1">hooks</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span>{<span class="pl-s">"before_tool"</span>: [<span class="pl-s1">audit_tool_calls</span>]},
)</pre></div>
<p>Built on this foundation:</p>
<ul>
<li><strong>Skills as first-class citizens</strong> — <code>SkillToolset</code> discovers skills through <strong>progressive disclosure</strong>, so the model sees only names and one-line descriptions until it loads one, keeping context lean.</li>
<li><strong>Dynamic tool selection at runtime</strong> — pass <code>tools=...</code> to <code>run</code> / <code>run_async</code> so one reusable Agent serves different teams, tenants, and tasks.</li>
<li><strong>Native async tools</strong> — <code>@tool</code> routes <code>async def</code> callables to a <code>Tool</code>'s new <code>async_function</code>.</li>
<li><strong>Human-in-the-Loop is now a <code>before_tool</code> hook</strong> (<code>ConfirmationHook</code>). <g-emoji class="g-emoji" alias="warning">⚠️</g-emoji></li>
<li><strong>Tool result offloading</strong> (<code>ToolResultOffloadHook</code>) writes large results to a store and leaves a compact pointer in the conversation.</li>
</ul>
<h3>🔎 Built-in introspection and observability</h3>
<p>Agents now expose <code>step_count</code>, <code>token_usage</code>, and <code>tool_call_counts</code> as built-in state. React to them to compact context when the window fills, cap runaway tool loops, or route to a cheaper approach past a budget threshold. Tracing now emits dedicated step-level spans (<code>haystack.agent.step</code> with nested <code>.llm</code> / <code>.tool</code> children) tagged with the tools actually used, so you can see exactly what your agent did. <g-emoji class="g-emoji" alias="warning">⚠️</g-emoji></p>
<h3>⚡ A core built for serving: first-class async</h3>
<p><code>Pipeline</code> and <code>AsyncPipeline</code> are now <strong>one class</strong> — no more switching between them. A single <code>Pipeline</code> exposes <code>run</code>, <code>run_async</code>, <code>run_async_generator</code>, and <code>stream</code>; serialized pipelines, <code>SuperComponent</code>s, and <code>PipelineTool</code>s load as <code>Pipeline</code> instances. <g-emoji class="g-emoji" alias="warning">⚠️</g-emoji></p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-python notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="from haystack import Pipeline

pipeline = Pipeline()
result = pipeline.run(data)                 # synchronous
result = await pipeline.run_async(data)     # asynchronous"><pre><span class="pl-k">from</span> <span class="pl-s1">haystack</span> <span class="pl-k">import</span> <span class="pl-v">Pipeline</span>

<span class="pl-s1">pipeline</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-en">Pipeline</span>()
<span class="pl-s1">result</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-s1">pipeline</span>.<span class="pl-c1">run</span>(<span class="pl-s1">data</span>)                 <span class="pl-c"># synchronous</span>
<span class="pl-s1">result</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-k">await</span> <span class="pl-s1">pipeline</span>.<span class="pl-c1">run_async</span>(<span class="pl-s1">data</span>)     <span class="pl-c"># asynchronous</span></pre></div>
<p>Concurrent tool calls and token-by-token streaming come standard: <code>Pipeline.stream()</code> yields <code>StreamingChunk</code>s as they're produced and exposes the final output on <code>handle.result</code> — the lower latency and streaming UX serving apps need. Components and <code>Pipeline</code> also get a symmetric lifecycle — <code>warm_up</code> to acquire, <code>close</code> to release — so long-running services don't leak connections, GPU memory, or file handles. External resources (and API keys) are now created at <code>warm_up</code>, not in <code>__init__</code>. <g-emoji class="g-emoji" alias="warning">⚠️</g-emoji></p>
<h3>🧱 A leaner, faster-moving framework <g-emoji class="g-emoji" alias="warning">⚠️</g-emoji></h3>
<p>Legacy Generators are gone, <code>haystack-experimental</code> is no longer a core dependency, and 30 components now live in independently released packages in <a href="https://github.com/deepset-ai/haystack-core-integrations">haystack-core-integrations</a>: Sentence Transformers, local &amp; API Hugging Face, Whisper, spaCy/langdetect, Tika and Azure OCR, SerperDev/SearchApi, OpenAPI connectors, and the Datadog/OpenTelemetry tracers. As a result, there are fewer transitive dependencies to audit and we can ship independent of the core release cycle. Migration is an install plus an import change:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-python notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="# pip install sentence-transformers-haystack
# Before: from haystack.components.embedders import SentenceTransformersTextEmbedder
from haystack_integrations.components.embedders.sentence_transformers import SentenceTransformersTextEmbedder"><pre><span class="pl-c"># pip install sentence-transformers-haystack</span>
<span class="pl-c"># Before: from haystack.components.embedders import SentenceTransformersTextEmbedder</span>
<span class="pl-k">from</span> <span class="pl-s1">haystack_integrations</span>.<span class="pl-s1">components</span>.<span class="pl-s1">embedders</span>.<span class="pl-s1">sentence_transformers</span> <span class="pl-k">import</span> <span class="pl-v">SentenceTransformersTextEmbedder</span></pre></div>
<p>The full old → new import table is in <a href="https://github.com/deepset-ai/haystack/blob/main/MIGRATION.md#components-moved-to-external-packages">MIGRATION.md</a>.</p>
<h3>💬 Chat Generators everywhere (legacy Generators removed) <g-emoji class="g-emoji" alias="warning">⚠️</g-emoji></h3>
<p><code>OpenAIGenerator</code>, <code>AzureOpenAIGenerator</code>, <code>HuggingFaceAPIGenerator</code>, and <code>HuggingFaceLocalGenerator</code> have been <strong>removed</strong> in favor of their Chat Generator counterparts. To ease the switch, all Chat Generators now accept a plain <code>str</code> for <code>messages</code>:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-python notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="# Before
from haystack.components.generators import OpenAIGenerator
text = OpenAIGenerator().run(&quot;What is NLP?&quot;)[&quot;replies&quot;][0]        # str

# After
from haystack.components.generators.chat import OpenAIChatGenerator
reply = OpenAIChatGenerator().run(&quot;What is NLP?&quot;)[&quot;replies&quot;][0]    # ChatMessage
text = reply.text                                                 # str; metadata in reply.meta"><pre><span class="pl-c"># Before</span>
<span class="pl-k">from</span> <span class="pl-s1">haystack</span>.<span class="pl-s1">components</span>.<span class="pl-s1">generators</span> <span class="pl-k">import</span> <span class="pl-v">OpenAIGenerator</span>
<span class="pl-s1">text</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-en">OpenAIGenerator</span>().<span class="pl-c1">run</span>(<span class="pl-s">"What is NLP?"</span>)[<span class="pl-s">"replies"</span>][<span class="pl-c1">0</span>]        <span class="pl-c"># str</span>

<span class="pl-c"># After</span>
<span class="pl-k">from</span> <span class="pl-s1">haystack</span>.<span class="pl-s1">components</span>.<span class="pl-s1">generators</span>.<span class="pl-s1">chat</span> <span class="pl-k">import</span> <span class="pl-v">OpenAIChatGenerator</span>
<span class="pl-s1">reply</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-en">OpenAIChatGenerator</span>().<span class="pl-c1">run</span>(<span class="pl-s">"What is NLP?"</span>)[<span class="pl-s">"replies"</span>][<span class="pl-c1">0</span>]    <span class="pl-c"># ChatMessage</span>
<span class="pl-s1">text</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-s1">reply</span>.<span class="pl-c1">text</span>                                                 <span class="pl-c"># str; metadata in reply.meta</span></pre></div>
<p><code>DALLEImageGenerator</code> was also renamed to <code>OpenAIImageGenerator</code>. <g-emoji class="g-emoji" alias="warning">⚠️</g-emoji></p>
<h3>🔒 Safer pipeline loading <g-emoji class="g-emoji" alias="warning">⚠️</g-emoji></h3>
<p>Pipeline deserialization (<code>Pipeline.load</code> / <code>loads</code> / <code>from_dict</code>) is now gated by a <strong>trusted-module allowlist</strong> (<code>haystack</code>, <code>haystack_integrations</code>, <code>haystack_experimental</code>, <code>builtins</code>, <code>typing</code>, <code>collections</code> by default), and dangerous builtins like <code>eval</code>, <code>exec</code>, <code>open</code>, and <code>getattr</code> can no longer be resolved. Allowlist custom modules when needed:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-python notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="Pipeline.load(fp, allowed_modules=[&quot;mypkg.*&quot;])   # or HAYSTACK_DESERIALIZATION_ALLOWLIST / allow_deserialization_module(...)
Pipeline.load(fp, unsafe=True)                    # only if you fully trust the source"><pre><span class="pl-v">Pipeline</span>.<span class="pl-c1">load</span>(<span class="pl-s1">fp</span>, <span class="pl-s1">allowed_modules</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span>[<span class="pl-s">"mypkg.*"</span>])   <span class="pl-c"># or HAYSTACK_DESERIALIZATION_ALLOWLIST / allow_deserialization_module(...)</span>
<span class="pl-v">Pipeline</span>.<span class="pl-c1">load</span>(<span class="pl-s1">fp</span>, <span class="pl-s1">unsafe</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-c1">True</span>)                    <span class="pl-c"># only if you fully trust the source</span></pre></div>
<h3>🧪 Testing without the API bill</h3>
<p>Newly added mock components mean no API keys, no network, no flaky CI for testing and prototyping. <code>MockChatGenerator</code> returns predefined responses, <code>MockTextEmbedder</code> and <code>MockDocumentEmbedder</code> return stable, hash-derived embeddings.</p>
<h3>A few smaller breaking changes to know about</h3>
<ul>
<li><strong>Required template variables by default</strong> — <code>Agent</code>, <code>PromptBuilder</code>, and <code>ChatPromptBuilder</code> now treat every Jinja2 variable as required (<code>required_variables=None</code> restores the old behavior); a new <code>{% insert %}</code> tag interleaves runtime messages. <g-emoji class="g-emoji" alias="warning">⚠️</g-emoji></li>
<li><strong>No more implicit <code>State</code> injection by name</strong> — tools must declare <code>inputs_from_state</code> explicitly to read a <code>State</code> value; same-named parameters no longer fill in automatically. <g-emoji class="g-emoji" alias="warning">⚠️</g-emoji></li>
<li><strong>Deterministic <code>Document.id</code></strong> — computed from canonical, key-sorted JSON of <code>meta</code>, so documents with non-empty <code>meta</code> get different IDs than in 2.x. <g-emoji class="g-emoji" alias="warning">⚠️</g-emoji></li>
<li><strong>Scoped logging</strong> — <code>configure_logging</code> attaches to Haystack's own loggers; importing Haystack no longer reconfigures <code>structlog</code> process-wide. <g-emoji class="g-emoji" alias="warning">⚠️</g-emoji></li>
<li><strong>Explicit tracing</strong> — no longer auto-enabled; add an <code>OpenTelemetryConnector</code> / <code>DatadogConnector</code> or call <code>tracing.enable_tracing(...)</code>. <g-emoji class="g-emoji" alias="warning">⚠️</g-emoji></li>
</ul>
<p>See the <strong>Upgrade Notes</strong> below for the full list.</p>
<h2>⬆️ Upgrade Notes</h2>
<ul>
<li>
<p><code>AsyncPipeline</code> has been removed. Its asynchronous capabilities are now part of the single <code>Pipeline</code> class, which provides both a synchronous <code>run</code> method and the asynchronous <code>run_async</code>, <code>run_async_generator</code>, and <code>stream</code> methods (mirroring how components expose <code>run</code> and <code>run_async</code>).</p>
<p>To migrate, replace <code>AsyncPipeline</code> with <code>Pipeline</code>:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-python notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="# Before
from haystack import AsyncPipeline
pipeline = AsyncPipeline()
result = await pipeline.run_async(data)

# After
from haystack import Pipeline
pipeline = Pipeline()
result = await pipeline.run_async(data)"><pre><span class="pl-c"># Before</span>
<span class="pl-k">from</span> <span class="pl-s1">haystack</span> <span class="pl-k">import</span> <span class="pl-v">AsyncPipeline</span>
<span class="pl-s1">pipeline</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-en">AsyncPipeline</span>()
<span class="pl-s1">result</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-k">await</span> <span class="pl-s1">pipeline</span>.<span class="pl-c1">run_async</span>(<span class="pl-s1">data</span>)

<span class="pl-c"># After</span>
<span class="pl-k">from</span> <span class="pl-s1">haystack</span> <span class="pl-k">import</span> <span class="pl-v">Pipeline</span>
<span class="pl-s1">pipeline</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-en">Pipeline</span>()
<span class="pl-s1">result</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-k">await</span> <span class="pl-s1">pipeline</span>.<span class="pl-c1">run_async</span>(<span class="pl-s1">data</span>)</pre></div>
<p>Serialized pipelines, <code>SuperComponent</code>s, and <code>PipelineTool</code>s are loaded as <code>Pipeline</code> instances.</p>
<p><code>Pipeline.run</code> runs synchronously and blocks until completion; in an async context use <code>await pipeline.run_async(...)</code>. The former <code>AsyncPipeline.run</code> was a synchronous wrapper around the concurrent engine, so to preserve that behavior use <code>asyncio.run(pipeline.run_async(...))</code>. Note that <code>Pipeline.run</code> executes components sequentially and does not accept <code>concurrency_limit</code>.</p>
<p>Both synchronous and asynchronous runs are traced under a single <code>haystack.pipeline.run</code> operation name, distinguished by a <code>haystack.pipeline.execution_mode</code> tag (<code>sync</code> or <code>async</code>). Previously, asynchronous runs used the <code>haystack.async_pipeline.run</code> operation name.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p><code>continue_run</code> is now a reserved key in <code>Agent.state_schema</code> (set by an <code>on_exit</code> hook to keep the Agent running). Passing it via the <code>state_schema</code> argument now raises <code>ValueError</code>. Rename any conflicting state key (e.g. <code>my_continue_run</code>) to migrate.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p><code>Agent</code> no longer validates <code>exit_conditions</code> against tool names at initialization. Exit conditions are now evaluated at runtime, so a condition naming a tool that is loaded later — e.g. a tool passed at runtime or one discovered by <code>SearchableToolset</code> — works as expected. Consequently, an unknown exit-condition name no longer raises a <code>ValueError</code> at construction.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Tool support is now validated against the tools resolved for each run, not only the tools provided at initialization. Passing tools at runtime via <code>run(tools=...)</code> to a chat generator that does not support tools now raises a <code>TypeError</code> instead of silently ignoring them.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p><code>Agent</code> now treats every Jinja2 template variable in <code>user_prompt</code> and <code>system_prompt</code> as <strong>required by default</strong>. The default of the <code>required_variables</code> init parameter has changed from <code>None</code> (all variables optional) to <code>"*"</code> (all variables required). This aligns <code>Agent</code> with <code>LLM</code>, <code>PromptBuilder</code>, and <code>ChatPromptBuilder</code>.</p>
<p>To check if you're affec...</p>
</li>
</ul></div>

        <div data-pjax="true" data-test-selector="body-content" data-view-component="true" class="markdown-body tmp-my-3"><h2>⭐️ Highlights</h2>
<h3>📦 Slimming down Haystack core ahead of 3.0</h3>
<p>This release begins the migration of many components out of <code>haystack</code> core and into dedicated integration packages, in preparation for Haystack 3.0. Components with heavy or optional dependencies — including all <code>SentenceTransformers</code> embedders and rankers, the Hugging Face API components, the legacy <code>Generator</code>s, <code>TikaDocumentConverter</code>, <code>AzureOCRDocumentConverter</code>, the Whisper transcribers, the OpenAPI connectors, the spaCy and Transformers extractors/classifiers/routers, <code>SerperDevWebSearch</code>, <code>SearchApiWebSearch</code>, <code>DocumentLanguageClassifier</code>/<code>TextLanguageRouter</code>, and the Datadog and OpenTelemetry tracers — are now deprecated and will be removed in 3.0.</p>
<p>Each component continues to work as before for now, and moving to the new package is a one-line import change once you install it. See the Deprecation Notes below for the full list and per-component migration snippets. For example:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-python notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="# Before
from haystack.components.embedders import SentenceTransformersTextEmbedder

# After: pip install sentence-transformers-haystack
from haystack_integrations.components.embedders.sentence_transformers import SentenceTransformersTextEmbedder"><pre><span class="pl-c"># Before</span>
<span class="pl-k">from</span> <span class="pl-s1">haystack</span>.<span class="pl-s1">components</span>.<span class="pl-s1">embedders</span> <span class="pl-k">import</span> <span class="pl-v">SentenceTransformersTextEmbedder</span>

<span class="pl-c"># After: pip install sentence-transformers-haystack</span>
<span class="pl-k">from</span> <span class="pl-s1">haystack_integrations</span>.<span class="pl-s1">components</span>.<span class="pl-s1">embedders</span>.<span class="pl-s1">sentence_transformers</span> <span class="pl-k">import</span> <span class="pl-v">SentenceTransformersTextEmbedder</span></pre></div>
<h3>🔀 Type-preserving routing with <code>ConditionalRouter</code></h3>
<p><code>ConditionalRouter</code> now accepts an <code>output_passthrough: True</code> flag on a route. When set, the route's <code>output</code> is treated as a plain variable name rather than a Jinja2 template, and the value is passed straight through from the pipeline inputs. This lets you route complex, non-basic types such as dataclasses and Pydantic models without the Jinja2 rendering silently converting them to their string representation.</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-python notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="from haystack.components.routers import ConditionalRouter

routes = [
    {
        &quot;condition&quot;: &quot;{{query.intent == 'search'}}&quot;,
        &quot;output&quot;: &quot;query&quot;,            # variable name, not a Jinja2 template
        &quot;output_name&quot;: &quot;search_query&quot;,
        &quot;output_type&quot;: ParsedQuery,
        &quot;output_passthrough&quot;: True,
    },
]

router = ConditionalRouter(routes)
result = router.run(query=query)
assert result[&quot;search_query&quot;] is query   # same object, type preserved"><pre><span class="pl-k">from</span> <span class="pl-s1">haystack</span>.<span class="pl-s1">components</span>.<span class="pl-s1">routers</span> <span class="pl-k">import</span> <span class="pl-v">ConditionalRouter</span>

<span class="pl-s1">routes</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> [
    {
        <span class="pl-s">"condition"</span>: <span class="pl-s">"{{query.intent == 'search'}}"</span>,
        <span class="pl-s">"output"</span>: <span class="pl-s">"query"</span>,            <span class="pl-c"># variable name, not a Jinja2 template</span>
        <span class="pl-s">"output_name"</span>: <span class="pl-s">"search_query"</span>,
        <span class="pl-s">"output_type"</span>: <span class="pl-v">ParsedQuery</span>,
        <span class="pl-s">"output_passthrough"</span>: <span class="pl-c1">True</span>,
    },
]

<span class="pl-s1">router</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-en">ConditionalRouter</span>(<span class="pl-s1">routes</span>)
<span class="pl-s1">result</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-s1">router</span>.<span class="pl-c1">run</span>(<span class="pl-s1">query</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-s1">query</span>)
<span class="pl-k">assert</span> <span class="pl-s1">result</span>[<span class="pl-s">"search_query"</span>] <span class="pl-c1">is</span> <span class="pl-s1">query</span>   <span class="pl-c"># same object, type preserved</span></pre></div>
<h2>⬆️ Upgrade Notes</h2>
<ul>
<li><code>DocumentNDCGEvaluator</code> now matches documents by their <code>content</code> field by default instead of their auto-generated <code>id</code>. Previously, ground truth and retrieved documents were matched only if they had identical <code>id</code> values, which rarely happened in practice since IDs are generated independently for each Document instance. As a result, NDCG scores computed with this evaluator may change for existing pipelines. To keep the previous <code>id</code>-based matching behavior, pass <code>document_comparison_field="id"</code> when constructing the evaluator.</li>
</ul>
<h2>🚀 New Features</h2>
<ul>
<li>Added native asynchronous support (<code>run_async</code>) to <code>LLMEvaluator</code>, <code>FaithfulnessEvaluator</code>, and <code>ContextRelevanceEvaluator</code>. This allows concurrent evaluation loops inside async applications like FastMCP or FastAPI without blocking the main event loop, while automatically falling back to thread workers for synchronous chat generators.</li>
<li>Added optional YAML frontmatter extraction to <code>MarkdownToDocument</code>. When initialized with <code>extract_frontmatter=True</code>, YAML frontmatter at the beginning of a Markdown file is removed from the converted content and added to <code>Document.meta</code>.</li>
</ul>
<h2>⚡️ Enhancement Notes</h2>
<ul>
<li>
<p>Added <code>document_comparison_field</code> parameter to <code>DocumentNDCGEvaluator</code>, consistent with <code>DocumentMAPEvaluator</code>, <code>DocumentMRREvaluator</code>, and <code>DocumentRecallEvaluator</code>. Users can now match documents by <code>"content"</code>, <code>"id"</code>, or any <code>"meta.&lt;key&gt;"</code> field when calculating NDCG scores.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Add <code>output_passthrough</code> option to <code>ConditionalRouter</code>. When <code>output_passthrough: True</code> is set in a route, the <code>output</code> field is treated as a plain variable name instead of a Jinja2 template, and the value is passed directly from the pipeline inputs to the route output. This allows routing of complex non-basic types such as dataclasses and Pydantic models without unwanted Jinja2 template processing.</p>
<p>Without <code>output_passthrough</code>, the router renders <code>output</code> as a Jinja2 template, which converts the value to its string representation. Custom types cannot survive that round-trip:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-python notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="# Without output_passthrough — the object is silently converted to a string
routes = [
    {
        &quot;condition&quot;: &quot;{{True}}&quot;,
        &quot;output&quot;: &quot;{{query}}&quot;,
        &quot;output_name&quot;: &quot;out&quot;,
        &quot;output_type&quot;: ParsedQuery,
    }
]
router = ConditionalRouter(routes)
result = router.run(query=ParsedQuery(text=&quot;hello&quot;, intent=&quot;search&quot;, entities=[]))
# result[&quot;out&quot;] == &quot;ParsedQuery(text='hello', intent='search', entities=[])&quot;
# ^^^ str, not ParsedQuery — the object was destroyed"><pre><span class="pl-c"># Without output_passthrough — the object is silently converted to a string</span>
<span class="pl-s1">routes</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> [
    {
        <span class="pl-s">"condition"</span>: <span class="pl-s">"{{True}}"</span>,
        <span class="pl-s">"output"</span>: <span class="pl-s">"{{query}}"</span>,
        <span class="pl-s">"output_name"</span>: <span class="pl-s">"out"</span>,
        <span class="pl-s">"output_type"</span>: <span class="pl-v">ParsedQuery</span>,
    }
]
<span class="pl-s1">router</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-en">ConditionalRouter</span>(<span class="pl-s1">routes</span>)
<span class="pl-s1">result</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-s1">router</span>.<span class="pl-c1">run</span>(<span class="pl-s1">query</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-en">ParsedQuery</span>(<span class="pl-s1">text</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-s">"hello"</span>, <span class="pl-s1">intent</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-s">"search"</span>, <span class="pl-s1">entities</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span>[]))
<span class="pl-c"># result["out"] == "ParsedQuery(text='hello', intent='search', entities=[])"</span>
<span class="pl-c"># ^^^ str, not ParsedQuery — the object was destroyed</span></pre></div>
<p>Set <code>output_passthrough: True</code> to skip Jinja2 entirely and pass the value directly from kwargs:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-python notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="from haystack.components.routers import ConditionalRouter
from dataclasses import dataclass, field

@dataclass
class ParsedQuery:
    text: str
    intent: str        # &quot;search&quot; | &quot;chat&quot;
    entities: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)

routes = [
    {
        &quot;condition&quot;: &quot;{{query.intent == 'search'}}&quot;,
        &quot;output&quot;: &quot;query&quot;,           # variable name, not a Jinja2 template
        &quot;output_name&quot;: &quot;search_query&quot;,
        &quot;output_type&quot;: ParsedQuery,
        &quot;output_passthrough&quot;: True,
    },
    {
        &quot;condition&quot;: &quot;{{query.intent == 'chat'}}&quot;,
        &quot;output&quot;: &quot;query&quot;,
        &quot;output_name&quot;: &quot;chat_query&quot;,
        &quot;output_type&quot;: ParsedQuery,
        &quot;output_passthrough&quot;: True,
    },
]

router = ConditionalRouter(routes)
query = ParsedQuery(text=&quot;What is Haystack?&quot;, intent=&quot;search&quot;, entities=[&quot;Haystack&quot;])
result = router.run(query=query)

assert isinstance(result[&quot;search_query&quot;], ParsedQuery)  # type preserved
assert result[&quot;search_query&quot;] is query                  # same object, no copying"><pre><span class="pl-k">from</span> <span class="pl-s1">haystack</span>.<span class="pl-s1">components</span>.<span class="pl-s1">routers</span> <span class="pl-k">import</span> <span class="pl-v">ConditionalRouter</span>
<span class="pl-k">from</span> <span class="pl-s1">dataclasses</span> <span class="pl-k">import</span> <span class="pl-s1">dataclass</span>, <span class="pl-s1">field</span>

<span class="pl-en">@<span class="pl-s1">dataclass</span></span>
<span class="pl-k">class</span> <span class="pl-v">ParsedQuery</span>:
    <span class="pl-s1">text</span>: <span class="pl-smi">str</span>
    <span class="pl-s1">intent</span>: <span class="pl-smi">str</span>        <span class="pl-c"># "search" | "chat"</span>
    <span class="pl-s1">entities</span>: <span class="pl-s1">list</span>[<span class="pl-smi">str</span>] <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-en">field</span>(<span class="pl-s1">default_factory</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-s1">list</span>)

<span class="pl-s1">routes</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> [
    {
        <span class="pl-s">"condition"</span>: <span class="pl-s">"{{query.intent == 'search'}}"</span>,
        <span class="pl-s">"output"</span>: <span class="pl-s">"query"</span>,           <span class="pl-c"># variable name, not a Jinja2 template</span>
        <span class="pl-s">"output_name"</span>: <span class="pl-s">"search_query"</span>,
        <span class="pl-s">"output_type"</span>: <span class="pl-v">ParsedQuery</span>,
        <span class="pl-s">"output_passthrough"</span>: <span class="pl-c1">True</span>,
    },
    {
        <span class="pl-s">"condition"</span>: <span class="pl-s">"{{query.intent == 'chat'}}"</span>,
        <span class="pl-s">"output"</span>: <span class="pl-s">"query"</span>,
        <span class="pl-s">"output_name"</span>: <span class="pl-s">"chat_query"</span>,
        <span class="pl-s">"output_type"</span>: <span class="pl-v">ParsedQuery</span>,
        <span class="pl-s">"output_passthrough"</span>: <span class="pl-c1">True</span>,
    },
]

<span class="pl-s1">router</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-en">ConditionalRouter</span>(<span class="pl-s1">routes</span>)
<span class="pl-s1">query</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-en">ParsedQuery</span>(<span class="pl-s1">text</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-s">"What is Haystack?"</span>, <span class="pl-s1">intent</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-s">"search"</span>, <span class="pl-s1">entities</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span>[<span class="pl-s">"Haystack"</span>])
<span class="pl-s1">result</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-s1">router</span>.<span class="pl-c1">run</span>(<span class="pl-s1">query</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-s1">query</span>)

<span class="pl-k">assert</span> <span class="pl-en">isinstance</span>(<span class="pl-s1">result</span>[<span class="pl-s">"search_query"</span>], <span class="pl-v">ParsedQuery</span>)  <span class="pl-c"># type preserved</span>
<span class="pl-k">assert</span> <span class="pl-s1">result</span>[<span class="pl-s">"search_query"</span>] <span class="pl-c1">is</span> <span class="pl-s1">query</span>                  <span class="pl-c"># same object, no copying</span></pre></div>
</li>
<li>
<p>Added an opt-in <code>expand_reference_ranges</code> parameter to <code>AnswerBuilder</code>. When enabled, reference ranges like <code>[6-10]</code> and comma-separated ranges like <code>[1-3,7-9]</code> are expanded to the corresponding document indices in RAG answers. The feature is disabled by default to preserve existing parsing behavior.</p>
</li>
</ul>
<h2><g-emoji class="g-emoji" alias="warning">⚠️</g-emoji> Deprecation Notes</h2>
<ul>
<li>
<p><code>AzureOCRDocumentConverter</code> is deprecated and will be removed from Haystack in version 3.0. It is moving to the <code>azure-form-recognizer-haystack</code> package. To continue using it, install the package with <code>pip install azure-form-recognizer-haystack</code> and update your import as follows:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-python notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="from haystack_integrations.components.converters.azure_form_recognizer import AzureOCRDocumentConverter"><pre><span class="pl-k">from</span> <span class="pl-s1">haystack_integrations</span>.<span class="pl-s1">components</span>.<span class="pl-s1">converters</span>.<span class="pl-s1">azure_form_recognizer</span> <span class="pl-k">import</span> <span class="pl-v">AzureOCRDocumentConverter</span></pre></div>
</li>
<li>
<p><code>DatadogTracer</code> is deprecated and will be removed from Haystack in version 3.0. It is moving to the <code>datadog-haystack</code> package. To continue using it, install the package with <code>pip install datadog-haystack</code> and add the <code>DatadogConnector</code> component to your pipeline to enable tracing, or update your import as follows:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-python notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="from haystack_integrations.tracing.datadog import DatadogTracer"><pre><span class="pl-k">from</span> <span class="pl-s1">haystack_integrations</span>.<span class="pl-s1">tracing</span>.<span class="pl-s1">datadog</span> <span class="pl-k">import</span> <span class="pl-v">DatadogTracer</span></pre></div>
<p>Note that, starting with Haystack 3.0, Datadog tracing is no longer auto-enabled when <code>ddtrace</code> is installed. Use the <code>DatadogConnector</code> component to enable it.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p><code>OpenAIGenerator</code>, <code>AzureOpenAIGenerator</code>, <code>HuggingFaceAPIGenerator</code>, and <code>HuggingFaceLocalGenerator</code> have been deprecated and will be removed in Haystack 3.0. Generators living in Haystack Core Integrations will also be removed in Haystack 3.0.</p>
<p>Their chat counterparts (<code>OpenAIChatGenerator</code>, <code>AzureOpenAIChatGenerator</code>, <code>HuggingFaceAPIChatGenerator</code>, <code>HuggingFaceLocalChatGenerator</code>) are the replacement. Starting from Haystack 2.30.0, all ChatGenerators also accept a plain <code>str</code> as input to make the transition easier.</p>
<p><strong>How to migrate:</strong></p>
<p><strong>Direct usage (running a generator from Python code)</strong></p>
<p>Before:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-python notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="from haystack.components.generators import OpenAIGenerator

gen = OpenAIGenerator()
result = gen.run(&quot;What is NLP?&quot;)
text = result[&quot;replies&quot;][0]   # str
meta = result[&quot;meta&quot;][0]      # dict with model metadata"><pre><span class="pl-k">from</span> <span class="pl-s1">haystack</span>.<span class="pl-s1">components</span>.<span class="pl-s1">generators</span> <span class="pl-k">import</span> <span class="pl-v">OpenAIGenerator</span>

<span class="pl-s1">gen</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-en">OpenAIGenerator</span>()
<span class="pl-s1">result</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-s1">gen</span>.<span class="pl-c1">run</span>(<span class="pl-s">"What is NLP?"</span>)
<span class="pl-s1">text</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-s1">result</span>[<span class="pl-s">"replies"</span>][<span class="pl-c1">0</span>]   <span class="pl-c"># str</span>
<span class="pl-s1">meta</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-s1">result</span>[<span class="pl-s">"meta"</span>][<span class="pl-c1">0</span>]      <span class="pl-c"># dict with model metadata</span></pre></div>
<p>After:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-python notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="from haystack.components.generators.chat import OpenAIChatGenerator

gen = OpenAIChatGenerator()
result = gen.run(&quot;What is NLP?&quot;)   # str input accepted directly
reply = result[&quot;replies&quot;][0]       # ChatMessage
text = reply.text                  # str
meta = reply.meta                  # dict with model metadata (now on the message)"><pre><span class="pl-k">from</span> <span class="pl-s1">haystack</span>.<span class="pl-s1">components</span>.<span class="pl-s1">generators</span>.<span class="pl-s1">chat</span> <span class="pl-k">import</span> <span class="pl-v">OpenAIChatGenerator</span>

<span class="pl-s1">gen</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-en">OpenAIChatGenerator</span>()
<span class="pl-s1">result</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-s1">gen</span>.<span class="pl-c1">run</span>(<span class="pl-s">"What is NLP?"</span>)   <span class="pl-c"># str input accepted directly</span>
<span class="pl-s1">reply</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-s1">result</span>[<span class="pl-s">"replies"</span>][<span class="pl-c1">0</span>]       <span class="pl-c"># ChatMessage</span>
<span class="pl-s1">text</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-s1">reply</span>.<span class="pl-c1">text</span>                  <span class="pl-c"># str</span>
<span class="pl-s1">meta</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-s1">reply</span>.<span class="pl-c1">meta</span>                  <span class="pl-c"># dict with model metadata (now on the message)</span></pre></div>
<p><strong>System prompt</strong></p>
<p>Before:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-python notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="from haystack.components.generators import OpenAIGenerator

gen = OpenAIGenerator(system_prompt=&quot;You are concise.&quot;)
result = gen.run(&quot;What is NLP?&quot;)"><pre><span class="pl-k">from</span> <span class="pl-s1">haystack</span>.<span class="pl-s1">components</span>.<span class="pl-s1">generators</span> <span class="pl-k">import</span> <span class="pl-v">OpenAIGenerator</span>

<span class="pl-s1">gen</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-en">OpenAIGenerator</span>(<span class="pl-s1">system_prompt</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-s">"You are concise."</span>)
<span class="pl-s1">result</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-s1">gen</span>.<span class="pl-c1">run</span>(<span class="pl-s">"What is NLP?"</span>)</pre></div>
<p>After:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-python notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="from haystack.components.generators.chat import OpenAIChatGenerator
from haystack.dataclasses import ChatMessage

gen = OpenAIChatGenerator()
result = gen.run([
    ChatMessage.from_system(&quot;You are concise.&quot;),
    ChatMessage.from_user(&quot;What is NLP?&quot;),

    <span class="pl-v">ChatMessage</span>.<span class="pl-c1">from_user</span>(<span class="pl-s">"What is NLP?"</span>),
])</pre></div>
<p><strong>Pipeline usage</strong></p>
<p>Pipelines that connected <code>PromptBuilder</code> (output: <code>str</code>) to a legacy Generator continue to work unchanged when you swap in a ChatGenerator. The Haystack pipeline type system automatically converts <code>str</code> to <code>list[ChatMessage]</code> at the connection edge.</p>
<p>Before:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-python notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="from haystack.components.generators import OpenAIGenerator
from haystack.components.builders import PromptBuilder

pipeline.add_component(&quot;prompt_builder&quot;, PromptBuilder(template=prompt_template))
pipeline.add_component(&quot;llm&quot;, OpenAIGenerator())
pipeline.connect(&quot;prompt_builder&quot;, &quot;llm&quot;)   # str -&gt; str"><pre><span class="pl-k">from</span> <span class="pl-s1">haystack</span>.<span class="pl-s1">components</span>.<span class="pl-s1">generators</span> <span class="pl-k">import</span> <span class="pl-v">OpenAIGenerator</span>
<span class="pl-k">from</span> <span class="pl-s1">haystack</span>.<span class="pl-s1">components</span>.<span class="pl-s1">builders</span> <span class="pl-k">import</span> <span class="pl-v">PromptBuilder</span>

<span class="pl-s1">pipeline</span>.<span class="pl-c1">add_component</span>(<span class="pl-s">"prompt_builder"</span>, <span class="pl-en">PromptBuilder</span>(<span class="pl-s1">template</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-s1">prompt_template</span>))
<span class="pl-s1">pipeline</span>.<span class="pl-c1">add_component</span>(<span class="pl-s">"llm"</span>, <span class="pl-en">OpenAIGenerator</span>())
<span class="pl-s1">pipeline</span>.<span class="pl-c1">connect</span>(<span class="pl-s">"prompt_builder"</span>, <span class="pl-s">"llm"</span>)   <span class="pl-c"># str -&gt; str</span></pre></div>
<p>After, minimal change (smart connection still works):</p>

from haystack.components.b..."><pre><span class="pl-k">from</span> <span class="pl-s1">haystack</span>.<span class="pl-s1">components</span>.<span class="pl-s1">generators</span>.<span class="pl-s1">chat</span> <span class="pl-k">import</span> <span class="pl-v">OpenAIChatGenerator</span>

        <div data-pjax="true" data-test-selector="body-content" data-view-component="true" class="markdown-body tmp-my-3"><h2>🐛 Bug Fixes</h2>
<ul>
<li>Fixed the <code>Agent</code> exiting prematurely under the default <code>exit_conditions=["text"]</code>. The agent now only stops when the last message is an assistant message with non-empty text (or when no tool invoker is configured). Previously, if the LLM produced an invalid tool call that was discarded, the resulting assistant message with empty text and no tool calls would trigger an exit, preventing the agent from recovering. The agent now continues looping so the model can recover on the next iteration.</li>
</ul></div>
</div>

        <div data-pjax="true" data-test-selector="body-content" data-view-component="true" class="markdown-body tmp-my-3"><h2>⚡️ Enhancement Notes</h2>
<ul>
<li><code>AzureOpenAIChatGenerator</code> now accepts a <code>Secret</code> for the <code>azure_endpoint</code> and <code>api_version</code> parameters in addition to a plain string. This makes it possible to resolve these values from environment variables at runtime, for example with <code>Secret.from_env_var("AZURE_OPENAI_ENDPOINT")</code>, so the same serialized pipeline can switch between environments (e.g. dev and prod) by changing environment variables instead of the pipeline definition.</li>
</ul></div>

    <path d="M4.855.708c.5-.896 1.79-.896 2.29 0l4.675 8.351a1.312 1.312 0 0 1-1.146 1.954H1.33A1.313 1.313 0 0 1 .183 9.058ZM7 7V3H5v4Zm-1 3a1 1 0 1 0 0-2 1 1 0 0 0 0 2Z"></path>

</remote-input></div>

          <a data-hovercard-type="commit" data-hovercard-url="/deepset-ai/haystack/commit/cdd105e1679ebe15d10a8411e4f59909a31b77fd/hovercard" href="/deepset-ai/haystack/commit/cdd105e1679ebe15d10a8411e4f59909a31b77fd" data-view-component="true" class="Link Link--muted"><svg aria-label="Commit" role="img" data-component="Octicon" height="16" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" data-view-component="true" class="octicon octicon-git-commit">

        <div data-pjax="true" data-test-selector="body-content" data-view-component="true" class="markdown-body tmp-my-3"><h2>⭐️ Highlights</h2>
<h3>🐍 Syntax-aware Python code splitting with <code>PythonCodeSplitter</code></h3>
<p>The new <code>PythonCodeSplitter</code> is a syntax-aware splitter for Python source files, built for code-RAG and code-search pipelines where naive line-based splitting tends to cut through functions and lose structural context. It parses sources with the <code>ast</code> module and greedily merges units, such as module docstring, import blocks, top-level functions, class headers, methods, and nested classes, into chunks of roughly <code>max_effective_lines</code>, keeping whole functions and methods together. For functions that exceed <code>oversized_factor * max_effective_lines</code>, it falls back to a line-based secondary split with overlap.</p>
<p>Two options make the resulting chunks more useful downstream: <code>strip_docstrings=True</code> moves docstrings into chunk metadata, and <code>preserve_class_definition=True</code> prepends the enclosing class signature to chunks whose members live in a later chunk. Each chunk also carries rich metadata including <code>start_line</code>, <code>end_line</code>, <code>unit_kinds</code>, <code>include_classes</code>, <code>decorators</code>, <code>docstrings</code>, <code>source_id</code>, and <code>split_id</code>.</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-python notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="from haystack.components.preprocessors import PythonCodeSplitter

splitter = PythonCodeSplitter(
    max_effective_lines=80,
    strip_docstrings=True,
    preserve_class_definition=True,
)
result = splitter.run(documents=[doc])"><pre><span class="pl-k">from</span> <span class="pl-s1">haystack</span>.<span class="pl-s1">components</span>.<span class="pl-s1">preprocessors</span> <span class="pl-k">import</span> <span class="pl-v">PythonCodeSplitter</span>

<span class="pl-s1">splitter</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-en">PythonCodeSplitter</span>(
    <span class="pl-s1">max_effective_lines</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-c1">80</span>,
    <span class="pl-s1">strip_docstrings</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-c1">True</span>,
    <span class="pl-s1">preserve_class_definition</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-c1">True</span>,
)
<span class="pl-s1">result</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-s1">splitter</span>.<span class="pl-c1">run</span>(<span class="pl-s1">documents</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span>[<span class="pl-s1">doc</span>])</pre></div>
<h3>💬 Pass a plain string to any <code>ChatGenerator</code></h3>
<p>All Haystack <code>ChatGenerator</code> components now accept a plain string for the <code>messages</code> parameter in addition to a list of <code>ChatMessage</code> objects. The string is automatically wrapped in a <code>ChatMessage</code> with the <code>user</code> role. This makes switching from a <code>Generator</code> to a <code>ChatGenerator</code> a one-line change. The change applies to <code>AzureOpenAIChatGenerator</code>, <code>AzureOpenAIResponsesChatGenerator</code>, <code>FallbackChatGenerator</code>, <code>HuggingFaceAPIChatGenerator</code>, <code>HuggingFaceLocalChatGenerator</code>, <code>OpenAIChatGenerator</code>, and <code>OpenAIResponsesChatGenerator</code>, and will soon be rolled out to the ChatGenerators in Haystack Core Integrations.</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-python notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="from haystack.components.generators.chat import OpenAIChatGenerator

generator = OpenAIChatGenerator()

# passing a string is equivalent to passing [ChatMessage.from_user(&quot;...&quot;)]
response = generator.run(&quot;What's Natural Language Processing?&quot;)
print(response[&quot;replies&quot;][0].text)"><pre><span class="pl-k">from</span> <span class="pl-s1">haystack</span>.<span class="pl-s1">components</span>.<span class="pl-s1">generators</span>.<span class="pl-s1">chat</span> <span class="pl-k">import</span> <span class="pl-v">OpenAIChatGenerator</span>

<span class="pl-s1">generator</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-en">OpenAIChatGenerator</span>()

<span class="pl-c"># passing a string is equivalent to passing [ChatMessage.from_user("...")]</span>
<span class="pl-s1">response</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-s1">generator</span>.<span class="pl-c1">run</span>(<span class="pl-s">"What's Natural Language Processing?"</span>)
<span class="pl-en">print</span>(<span class="pl-s1">response</span>[<span class="pl-s">"replies"</span>][<span class="pl-c1">0</span>].<span class="pl-c1">text</span>)</pre></div>
<h2>⬆️ Upgrade Notes</h2>
<ul>
<li>
<p><code>DALLEImageGenerator</code> has been updated to account for OpenAI's retirement of the DALL-E models. The default model is now <code>gpt-image-2</code> (previously <code>dall-e-3</code>). To migrate:</p>
<ul>
<li>Update <code>model</code> value: besides <code>gpt-image-2</code>, <code>gpt-image-1</code> and <code>gpt-image-1-mini</code> are also supported.</li>
<li>Update <code>quality</code> value: the new accepted values are <code>auto</code>, <code>high</code>, <code>medium</code>, or <code>low</code> (previously <code>standard</code> or <code>hd</code>).</li>
<li>Update <code>size</code> value: the new accepted values are <code>1024x1024</code>, <code>1024x1536</code>, <code>1536x1024</code>, or <code>auto</code>. <code>gpt-image-2</code> also supports arbitrary sizes.</li>
<li>The <code>response_format</code> parameter is now ignored. The component always returns base64-encoded JSON.</li>
</ul>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-python notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="# Before
llm.run([message], my_callback)

# After
llm.run(messages=[message], streaming_callback=my_callback)"><pre><span class="pl-c"># Before</span>
<span class="pl-s1">llm</span>.<span class="pl-c1">run</span>([<span class="pl-s1">message</span>], <span class="pl-s1">my_callback</span>)

<span class="pl-c"># After</span>
<span class="pl-s1">llm</span>.<span class="pl-c1">run</span>(<span class="pl-s1">messages</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span>[<span class="pl-s1">message</span>], <span class="pl-s1">streaming_callback</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-s1">my_callback</span>)</pre></div>
</li>
</ul>
<h2>🚀 New Features</h2>
<ul>
<li>
<p>Introduced the <code>PythonCodeSplitter</code> component, a syntax-aware splitter for Python source files:</p>
<ul>
<li>Parses sources with the <code>ast</code> module and merges units (module docstring, import blocks, top-level functions, class headers, methods, nested classes, and remaining statements) greedily into chunks of roughly <code>max_effective_lines</code>.</li>
<li>Keeps whole functions and methods together; falls back to a line-based secondary split (using <code>DocumentSplitter</code>) with overlap only for functions whose effective length exceeds <code>oversized_factor * max_effective_lines</code>.</li>
<li>Optionally strips docstrings into chunk metadata via <code>strip_docstrings=True</code>, and prepends the enclosing class signature to chunks whose members live in a later chunk via <code>preserve_class_definition=True</code>.</li>
<li>Emits per-chunk metadata including <code>start_line</code>, <code>end_line</code>, <code>unit_kinds</code>, <code>include_classes</code>, <code>decorators</code>, <code>docstrings</code>, <code>source_id</code>, and <code>split_id</code>.</li>
</ul>
</li>
<li>
<p>All Haystack <code>ChatGenerator</code> components now also accept a plain string for the <code>messages</code> parameter in addition to a list of <code>ChatMessage</code> objects. The string is automatically converted into a list containing a <code>ChatMessage</code> with the <code>user</code> role. This is done to simplify switching from Generators to ChatGenerators; Generators might be removed in Haystack 3.0.</p>
<p>This applies to <code>AzureOpenAIChatGenerator</code>, <code>AzureOpenAIResponsesChatGenerator</code>, <code>FallbackChatGenerator</code>, <code>HuggingFaceAPIChatGenerator</code>, <code>HuggingFaceLocalChatGenerator</code>, <code>OpenAIChatGenerator</code>, and <code>OpenAIResponsesChatGenerator</code>.</p>
<p>The same change will be soon applied to ChatGenerators available in Haystack Core Integrations.</p>
<p>Example:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-python notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="from haystack.components.generators.chat import OpenAIChatGenerator

generator = OpenAIChatGenerator()

# passing a string is equivalent to passing [ChatMessage.from_user(&quot;...&quot;)]
response = generator.run(&quot;What's Natural Language Processing?&quot;)
print(response[&quot;replies&quot;][0].text)"><pre><span class="pl-k">from</span> <span class="pl-s1">haystack</span>.<span class="pl-s1">components</span>.<span class="pl-s1">generators</span>.<span class="pl-s1">chat</span> <span class="pl-k">import</span> <span class="pl-v">OpenAIChatGenerator</span>

<span class="pl-s1">generator</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-en">OpenAIChatGenerator</span>()

<span class="pl-c"># passing a string is equivalent to passing [ChatMessage.from_user("...")]</span>
<span class="pl-s1">response</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-s1">generator</span>.<span class="pl-c1">run</span>(<span class="pl-s">"What's Natural Language Processing?"</span>)
<span class="pl-en">print</span>(<span class="pl-s1">response</span>[<span class="pl-s">"replies"</span>][<span class="pl-c1">0</span>].<span class="pl-c1">text</span>)</pre></div>
</li>
</ul>
<h2>⚡️ Enhancement Notes</h2>
<ul>
<li>Added <code>run_async</code> to <code>TextEmbeddingRetriever</code>, <code>MultiQueryEmbeddingRetriever</code>, and <code>MultiQueryTextRetriever</code>. These components now execute natively as coroutines in <code>AsyncPipeline</code>, delegating to each wrapped component's <code>run_async</code> when available and falling back to a thread executor otherwise.</li>
<li>Fix grammar in the <code>AzureOpenAIGenerator</code> and <code>AzureOpenAIChatGenerator</code> docstring code examples (<code>"&lt;this a model name..."</code> → <code>"&lt;this is a model name..."</code>) so that copy-pasted snippets read correctly.</li>
<li>Update <code>ToolsType</code> to improve type checking for the <code>tools</code> parameter. Any class that inherits from either <code>Tool</code> or <code>Toolset</code> is now accepted in any sequence (list, tuple, etc).</li>
<li><code>Pipeline.draw()</code> and <code>Pipeline.show()</code> now validate the Mermaid server response before writing it to disk. The response body is checked against the expected output format (PNG, JPEG, WebP, SVG, or PDF) via its magic-byte signature, and the <code>Content-Type</code> header is checked as well. If the response is empty or does not match the requested format, a <code>PipelineDrawingError</code> is raised and no file is written. This prevents a misconfigured or untrusted <code>server_url</code> from causing arbitrary content (for example an HTML error page) to be saved verbatim to the output path.</li>
</ul>
<h2>🐛 Bug Fixes</h2>
<ul>
<li>Prevent <code>Document.from_dict()</code> from mutating the input dictionary during deserialization.</li>
<li>Prevent DocumentLanguageClassifier from crashing when <code>Document.content=None</code> by marking them as unmatched and logging a warning.</li>
<li>Fixed a bug where <code>Agent</code> would not exit when the model emitted multiple tool calls in a single turn and the configured exit-condition tool was not the first one in the list. Previously, only the first tool call in each assistant message was checked against <code>exit_conditions</code>, so a reply like <code>[search, finish]</code> (with <code>exit_conditions=["finish"]</code>) would silently fail to stop the loop and keep iterating until <code>max_agent_steps</code> was reached. Since parallel tool calls are now the norm for frontier models, this could quietly turn a single successful turn into dozens of wasted LLM calls. The <code>Agent</code> now inspects every tool call in the message, so the exit condition is honored regardless of ordering.</li>
<li>Fix <code>AnswerBuilder.run()</code> mutating the <code>meta</code> dict of input <code>Document</code> objects. <code>source_index</code> (and <code>referenced</code> when <code>reference_pattern</code> is set) are now only added to the document copies inside <code>GeneratedAnswer.documents</code>, not to the originals.</li>
<li>Fixed <code>DocumentJoiner</code> in <code>concatenate</code> mode so that documents with a score of exactly <code>0.0</code> are no longer treated as unscored during deduplication. Previously a truthiness check coerced <code>score=0.0</code> to <code>-inf</code>, which could cause a worse, negatively-scored duplicate to be kept instead of the <code>0.0</code>-scored document. The <code>merge</code> mode was updated to the same explicit <code>is not None</code> check for consistency; its observable behavior is unchanged.</li>
<li>Fixed in-place mutation of <code>ExtractedAnswer.meta</code> in <code>ExtractiveReader._add_answer_page_number</code> when the answer's <code>meta</code> was <code>None</code>. Now uses <code>dataclasses.replace</code> to avoid triggering the dataclass mutation warning.</li>
<li>Fixed <code>ExtractiveReader</code> raising <code>ValueError</code> when the number of valid answer spans for a sequence was smaller than <code>answers_per_seq</code> (for example with short documents or when <code>answers_per_seq</code> exceeded the number of upper-triangular, non-masked (start, end) token pairs). <code>_postprocess</code> now filters the per-sequence probabilities by the same validity mask it already applied to the start/end token indices, so the three structures always have matching lengths.</li>
<li><code>HierarchicalDocumentSplitter</code> no longer mutates the metadata of the input <code>Document</code>. <code>_add_meta_data</code> now returns a new <code>Document</code> with a copied <code>meta</code> dict via <code>dataclasses.replace</code> instead of writing <code>__block_size</code>, <code>__parent_id</code>, <code>__children_ids</code> and <code>__level</code> onto the caller's <code>Document</code>.</li>
<li>Fixed a bug in <code>LLMMetadataExtractor.run_async</code> where the <code>asyncio.Semaphore</code> intended to bound concurrent LLM calls to <code>max_workers</code> was acquired once around the outer <code>gather(...)</code> call instead of inside each task. As a result, <code>max_workers</code> had no effect in <code>run_async</code> and all LLM requests for a batch were issued simultaneously. The semaphore is now acquired per task, so <code>max_workers</code> correctly caps in-flight requests.</li>
<li><code>expand_page_range()</code> now raises a <code>ValueError: too many values to unpack</code> when a page range string contained more than one hyphen (e.g. <code>"10-20-30"</code>). The parser now validates the format and raises a clear <code>ValueError</code> with an explanatory message for invalid inputs.</li>
<li><code>LLMMetadataExtractor</code> now raises a clear <code>ValueError</code> when the <code>prompt</code> contains no template variables. Previously this case raised an unhelpful <code>IndexError: list index out of range</code>. The error message now consistently expl...</li>
</ul></div>
          <a href="/deepset-ai/haystack/releases/tag/v2.30.0" data-view-component="true" class="Link">Read more</a>
</div>
